- Normalize S=1.
- At each step compute c and find peaks.
- Gradient of score w.r.t a_i for active peak k*: if max c_{k*}, derivative of c_{k*} w.r.t a_i is (if k* - i in range): a_{k*-i} + (if i != k*-i) a_{k*-i} else maybe same = basically 2 a_{k*-i} except half diagonal? More exactly c_k = sum_i a_i a_{k-i}. For real a, if k-i != i, the two terms are separate but derivative w.r.t a_i gets a_{k-i} from first when term i, and if there is j such that k-j=i i.e. j=k-i, derivative also a_j=a_{k-i}; if k-i=i then derivative 2a_i. So gradient vector g_k has entries: for i in [max(0,k-n+1),min(n-1,k)], g_i = a_{k-i} + (i != k-i)*a_{k-i} = 2a_{k-i} if i != k-i else a_i. Since a_{k-i} consistent. Thus gradient is 2*reverse slice of a (with middle half).
- Subgradient of max is gradient of one active peak (if multiple, average).
- But objective includes S^2; after normalization S fixed, minimize max c.

Let's design a practical iterative algorithm:
1. Maintain a positive vector (maybe add ε floor). Normalize sum=1 (or keep sum fixed 1 for simplicity). Score = 2n max c.
2. Smooth peakedness objective: L = Σ_k (c_k)^p or log-sum-exp: ((1/β) log Σ exp(β c_k)). But c_k can be large; scale to keep numerics. Since we fix S=1, c_k around 1/(2n) ~0.00011. We can scale by (2n-1) to make mean 1. Let d_k = (2n-1) c_k / S² (after S fixed 1, d=(2n-1)c). Then average 1, uniform max d≈2. Score = 2n max c = 2n/(2n-1) max d ≈ max d. So minimizing max d is key.
3. Use subgradient of log-sum-exp L_β = (1/β) log Σ exp(β d_k). Gradient for a_i can be computed as weighted sum of c gradients with softmax weights. d_k=(2n-1)c_k. ∂d_k/∂a_i = (2n-1)*∂c_k/∂a_i = 2(2n-1)*a_{k-i} for valid (half diag). Then gradient G = (1/β) Σ_k exp(β d_k)/Z * ∂d_k/∂a_i. This is a convolution of weight w_k=softmax(β d_k) with a (with factor 4n?). Specifically G_i ∝ Σ_k w_k a_{k-i}.
4. We want minimize L subject to a_i≥0, sum=1. Use projected gradient: a ← normalize(max(0, a - η G)). But G nonnegative, subtract reduces all. If normalize, total mass moves from high G to low G. G_i is sum of a at mirrored positions weighted by peak lags. This may help.

But with sum fixed and a nonnegative, subtracting G may zero out. Use multiplicative update: a_i ← exp(a_i - η G_i) or a_i ← a_i * exp(-η G_i / a_i?) We can use exponentiated gradient: a_i = exp(u_i - η G_i), then normalize. This keeps positive and allows zeros limit. The objective in u is not convex due to normalization but might work.

We can implement a flexible update:
- Maintain logu u (initial plus random noise or parametric).
- For each iteration:
  a = softmax(u) (normalized to sum 1). Use stable: m=u-max; exp; divide.
  c = fftconvolve(a,a) length 2n-1.
  score = 2n*max(c) (since S=1)
  if improved best, save.
  Compute weights w:
    Option A: logsumexp temperature beta: q = beta*(c - c.min()); w=exp(q); w/=sum.
    Option B: top-K: w zeros; w[topK]=1; maybe weighted by (c-top_low)^p.
  Could combine: w = softmax(beta*(c - mean)) and then sharpen: w**=γ? 
  For small β, weights broad, may optimize variance. For large, peaks.
  Compute H = valid_correlate(w,a).
  Q = dot(a,H) = Σ w_k c_k (can compute dot(w,c) cheaper).
  g = a * (H - Q)
  # natural gradient with learning rate lr. We need scale. H and Q are O(1/n). g O(1/n^2). For n=4500, ~5e-8. To get meaningful u changes, lr ~1e6? Could set lr = n^2 * step (e.g., 10). Use line search / backtracking:
- Given a and update direction g (natural gradient descent direction for u), consider u_new = u - step * g. But natural gradient direction may not be normalized. The gradient on u is a*..., but a factor is already in softmax? Let's be precise: For softmax parameterization, gradient descent step: u -= η ∇_u P = η a(g_a - a·g_a). If ∇_u = 2a(H-Q), update u -= 2η a(H-Q). If we omit a factor and update u -= η(H-Q), that corresponds to Fisher? The a factor makes update relative? Since δu_i proportional to a_i, relative log-odds change larger for large a. Maybe natural gradient often uses δu_i = g_a_i - a·g_a (no a), which yields Euclidean? For softmax, the metric is diag(a)-aa^T; the natural gradient is (I - 1a)g_a? Actually Fisher Riemannian gradient on probability simplex is (diag(a)-aa^T)^{-1} g_a, which has components? The inverse isn't simple except if update in natural parameter uses g_a? Let's recall: softmax map u -> p. Riemannian metric G=diag(p)-pp^T. Natural gradient η_nat = G^{-1} η_euc. In natural coordinates (u), Euclidean gradient η_win = p_i η_euc_i - p_i Σ p_j η_euc_j = p_i(η_euc_i - π). This is simply p_i times exponential family gradient difference. So our g_u = p_i (g_a_i - avg) is correct. For Q, g_a=2H, avg=2Q, so g_u=2p(H-Q). Thus δu_i = -η 2 p_i(H-Q). This is proportional to p_i, meaning relative change δp/p = δu. So update multiplicative: p_i ← p_i * exp(-η 2 p_i(H-Q)). That's possibly too small for small p. Maybe omit p_i: δu_i = -η (H-Q) (not multiplying p) is not natural gradient but "entropy" update? If δu not proportional p, small p change relatively more. Could be good to explore. But to follow gradient, include p.

Maybe use direct mirror descent with KL: p_new = normalize(exp(log p - η * 2(H-Q))). This is δu=-η2(H-Q) (no p factor). Is that gradient of Q with respect to log p? Exponential gradient of Q? ∂Q/∂log p_i = p_i ∂Q/∂p_i =2p_iH_i. Exponential gradient for minimization on p? Update p ∝ exp(-η ∂Q/∂log p) = exp(-η 2pH), not H. So not.

Natural gradient with p factor may be too conservative. We can use both: update u -= η * (H - Q) (no p) or u -= η * p*(H-Q). We can select via line search among variants.

Let's more deeply analyze natural gradient for weighted sum Q_w.
On simplex, Q_w = Σ_i,j M_{ij} a_i a_j with M_{ij}=w_{i+j}. Natural gradient flow da/dt = - (diag(a)-aa^T) G? Actually gradient step in u: du = -η a (g_a - a·g_a). Then da = a du = -η a² (g_a - a·g_a). Equivalent to multiplicative update: a_i ← a_i exp(-η a_i (g_a_i - avg)). Since g_a_i=2H_i, avg=2Q. So a_i ← a_i exp(-2η a_i (H_i-Q)). This update decreases a_i by factor depending on a_i H_i. For uniform and symmetric weights, maybe no change. If we include noise, it moves.

Line search procedure:
Given u, direction d (e.g., d=-(H-Q) for δu variant, or d=-a*(H-Q)). Normalize d by its l2 norm? We want try scalers s. We can compute candidate a = softmax(u + s*d) (if d already direction). But d scale ~1/n, l2 ~1/√n? s must be ~n. We can normalize d to unit RMS and use relative step λ: u_cand = u + λ * d_hat, where d_hat = d / rms(d). Then λ represents standard deviation of log changes. Try λ=[1e-3,1e-2,1e-1,0.3,1,3,10]? This is more stable. The direction sign matters. We can evaluate score for each λ and accept best if < current. This is a derivative-free line search using gradient direction. Good.

Algorithm phases:
Phase 0: Generate candidate initial sequences for selected N; compute score; keep best few.
Phase 1: Variance-refinement: For each good candidate, iterate with v=c-mean natural/exponential updates, line search, accept if score improves (or variance improves but prefer score). This can flatten and get score maybe ~1.7-1.9.
Phase 2: Peak-refinement: Use weights w = softmax(γ*(c-mean)/std) or top peaks; minimize peak smooth. Continue.
Phase 3: Random block perturbations / multiple starts to escape local minima.
Phase 4: Final coordinate/local search around best using evaluate_sequence or FFT.

Let's implement a powerful local optimizer that works for both block and full:
- Objective smooth: f(a)=logsumexp(beta*c) - 2*log(sum) (using stable; if sum small). But for block search, can use exact max but non-smooth; smoothing okay.
- Use L-BFGS-B? For B=128, many random starts possible. For full n=1024/2048, maybe one L-BFGS.
But implementing own natural gradient may be more efficient.

Let's define a function optimize_vector(a, budget, beta_schedule, method='natgrad'):
- a positive sum 1.
- u=log(a) (avoid zeros: a = np.maximum(a, eps)).
- For each iteration (time deadline):
   1. normalize a = softmax(u) if maintaining u. But if a has zeros, log -inf. Use a with floor eps for optimization; final allow zeros? Nonnegative allowed. We can keep positive floor to avoid issues; final clip.
   2. compute c = conv(a,a) (sum=1)
   3. score = 2n max(c). Update best.
   4. choose weights:
      a) variance: v = c - mean; norm_v maybe.
      b) peak: q = (c - mean)/std; w = softmax(beta_peak * q) (beta maybe adaptive). Or top K with weights = (c-thresh)_+.
   We can alternate: if score high relative, use peak; if variance high, use variance. Or combine v_total = v/||v|| + w/||w|| with sign.
   5. Compute H = valid_correlate(v_total, a). For signed v, H may be negative. Q=dot(v_total,c). Direction on logu: d = - (H - Q) or d= -a*(H-Q). Normalize d by std.
   6. Line search over λ in [0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3,1,3,10] maybe: u_new = u + λ*d_std? Since d is for u (direction). Compute a_new=softmax(u_new); score_new; accept best λ if improves current; else maybe smaller/random.
   7. Add random perturbation to u every K iterations with sigma annealed.

Need choose scaling of d. If d = H-Q, H and Q scale ~1/n, d std ~1/n. To get λ~1, u changes 1/n, too small. Normalize d_std = d / (std(d)+eps), then λ standard deviation of u. Use λ in [0.01,10]. Score change can be evaluated. This is robust.

But if we normalize variance vs peak, direction scale lost; fine.

Line search function expensive: each candidate requires softmax, convolution, score. Number candidates ~5. For n=2048, maybe 5* FFT (4096) per iter; okay 1000 iter? 5000 FFT maybe seconds to minutes. Need limit.

We can compute c for current, then for candidates need new c. Use numpy FFT each time. For smaller block B=128, trivial.

Let's refocus on algorithm. The continuous uniform may be optimal, but discrete finite may have small improvements. To reach 1.5, maybe need length modest and algorithm can find. Previous state-of-art LP from problem statement likely can get below 1.5 if implemented. Let's implement LP approach more seriously; it is designed for this problem and may find good sequences.

SOTA LP: start normalized f sum sqrt(2n). Compute M=||f*f||∞. Solve LP maximize sum g, g≥0, ||f*g||∞ ≤ M. Rescale g to sum sqrt(2n), update f←(1-t)f+t g. Repeat.
We noted from uniform, LP with M current may return uniform. But if start non-uniform or use stricter target T< M, can move. The LP is principled: it finds g whose cross-conv with f is bounded. If we set T = M - δ (or target peak), it forces g away from f at peaks. Then interpolation may reduce self-peak. We can implement with active constraints and use target T = (1 - step) M or even target based on desired score.

LP details:
Variables g ≥0. Constraints: (f*g)[k] ≤ T for k in active (maybe all top or all). Max sum g. The optimum will push g mass to places where f is small in peak lags. Then normalize g to sum same as f. Candidate = (1-t)*f + t*g.
This is a way to find a point in a "Cayley" set. It might produce large structural changes.

Can solve LP for n=1024/2048 with scipy HiGHS. Constraints active K maybe all 2n-1? Building 4M nnz for n=1024 (all) manageable. For repeated LP, maybe 50-200 iterations. Need choose K.
If use all constraints (2n-1), the LP is exactly SOTA. For n=1024, constraints 2047, vars1024; nnz ~1,048,576? Actually n² ~1M, okay. HiGHS can solve in <0.1s? Maybe 0.5s. 1800s -> thousands, but building matrix each time maybe. For n=2048, nnz~4M, maybe 1-2s per LP; 500 LP enough.

Can reuse constraint matrix structure; values change with f. Building sparse matrix values O(nnz). We can precompute indices for active set. For all constraints, rows/cols arrays length n²; for n=2048, 4M ints each ~32MB, okay. Multiple arrays maybe 100MB. To be safe use n=1024 for LP, or active subset.

LP with all constraints and target T. If T too low, infeasible. Need choose T such that feasible: f itself has cross f*f peak M. For T<M, f infeasible, but some g may exist. The maximum feasible T? If infeasible, increase T. Start T = current M * 0.9? Should be feasible likely. But LP maximize sum; as T decreases, sum_g decreases.

LP implementation details:
def lp_direction(f, T, active_indices, active_threshold=True):
    n=len(f)
    m=len(active)
    Build sparse A_ub for constraints rows active:
      For each row r_idx, global k=active[r_idx]. Columns j where j in [max(0,k-n+1), min(n-1,k)]. Values f[k-j].
    c = -ones(n)
    bounds=[(0, None)]*n maybe add (0, ub) to prevent spikes? But if ub too low infeasible. Let ub = 100 * S/n? f sum=1, values ~1/n; g before normalization can be up to? T~1/n, g values can be T at one var, ~0.001. ub=1 maybe safe. But after normalization sum=1, if g sum very small, spike 1. Bound not help after normalization. We can enforce minimum support by adding tiny lower bound? Nonnegative LP no. After g, we can mix with uniform to avoid one-hot: g = (1-ε)g + ε/n.
    res=linprog(c, A_ub, b_ub=T, bounds, method='highs')
    if success, g=res.x; if sum_g <=0 return None.
    g = g + eps_uniform? then normalize to sum S (1): g = g / sum_g *1.
    Optionally clip 0..1000.
   Return g.

Active set: Use all 2n-1? For n=1024, okay. But if using T<M, constraints all may be many. We can use all for first iterations, then top K to speed.
Build sparse matrix each LP. We can precompute row/col arrays for all active lags to speed. For all lags 0..2n-2, total nnz=n². For n=1024, 1,048,576; arrays of int64 8MB each, values float 8MB, okay. Building values each time: for each k, slice f[k-j]. We can vectorize loops over rows. Python loop over 2047 rows, each with up to 1024 appends, total 1M appends; might be slow (seconds). Need optimize.

Alternative build A_ub dense for n=1024? 2047x1024 = 2M floats =16MB, okay. Building dense via numpy broadcasting maybe faster? For each row k, A[k, j_start:j_end+1] = f[k-j_start:k-j_end-1]? Could loop 2047 rows, assigning slices. 2047* average 512 =1M assignments in C via slice, maybe ~0.01s? Good. Dense LP with HiGHS may be okay. But memory 16MB, plus solver copy maybe. For n=2048, 8MB? Actually 4095x2048=8.4M floats=67MB, okay maybe. Use n=1024 to be safe.

Dense constraint matrix for n=1024 can be built:
A = np.empty((2*n-1, n))
for k in range(2*n-1):
    j0=max(0,k-n+1); j1=min(n-1,k)
    length=j1-j0+1
    # values f[k - j] for j=j0..j1
    # indices k-j from k-j0 down to k-j1. Example k=3,n=5: j0=0,j1=3, need f[3],f[2],f[1],f[0]
    A[k, j0:j1+1] = f[k-j0 : k-j1-1 : -1]? Let's derive. j0=0,j1=3, slice f[3:0:-1] gives f[3],f[2],f[1]. Missing f[0] because stop exclusive 0. Need f[k-j0 : k-j1-1 : -1] if k-j1-1 >=0? For j1=3, k-j1-1=-1, stop -1 gives f[3: -1: -1] = f[3],f[2],f[1], not f[0]. If stop None? f[3:None:-1] gives f[3],f[2],f[1],f[0]. In general want f[k-j0], f[k-j0-1], ..., f[k-j1]. Use slice f[k-j0 : (k-j1)-1 : -1] if k-j1 >=0; if k-j1=0, stop -1? Python stop -1 means index -1 (last) not 0. Better use f[k-j0 : max(k-j1-1, -1) : -1]? If stop -1, for length ending 0, f[3:-1:-1] => [3,2,1], not 0. Use f[k-j0 : (k-j1)-1 if k-j1-1>=0 else None : -1]. If k-j1=0, stop None gives down to 0. If k-j1>0, stop k-j1-1.
    Or use np.take(f, range(k-j0, k-j1-1, -1)) but slower.
Simpler: Precompute for each k a list of source indices? Use row slice from f_rev? Let f_rev = f[::-1]. Then (f*g)[k] = Σ_{j} f[k-j] g_j. For j range, f indices k-j. If define h = f_rev, h[n-1-(k-j)] = f[k-j] = h[n-1-k+j]. This is a contiguous slice of h with step 1: indices n-1-k+j0 to n-1-k+j1. Thus A[k, j0:j1+1] = f_rev[n-1-k+j0 : n-1-k+j1+1]. Great! Let's verify n=5,k=3: f_rev index base=5-1-3+0=1, length4: f_rev[1:5] = f[3],f[2],f[1],f[0]? f_rev=[f4,f3,f2,f1,f0], indices1..4 = f3,f2,f1,f0 yes. Good.
So dense build:
fr = f[::-1]
for k in range(2*n-1):
  j0=max(0,k-n+1); j1=min(n-1,k); length=j1-j0+1
  start = n-1-k+j0
  A[k, j0:j1+1] = fr[start:start+length]
This is fast (2047 slice assignments). Dense A values are nonnegative.

However, for LP with b=T (same for all constraints), if T small, many constraints redundant? But okay.

HiGHS dense maybe slower but n=1024 okay. Could pass A_ub as float64. If time, we can use sparse if dense too slow. Let's choose n=512 for LP? 1024 better. Maybe n=1024 with all constraints works.

LP objective c=-np.ones(n). If no upper bounds, solution may be unbounded? Constraints with nonnegative A; if some column all zeros (no? every j appears in rows k=j..j+n-1; A positive if f slice includes zeros maybe could be zero if f zeros). If f has zeros, matrix may have zero columns, then variable with all zero constraints is unbounded objective -inf (max sum unbounded). Need handle: add upper bound ub for all variables. Choose ub = 1.0 (since f sum=1, g can be >? Cross constraints T≤M maybe ~0.001, but if f zeros allow unbounded; ub prevents). For f positive, A entries positive? If f positive, each variable appears in constraints with positive values, bounded. But due to numerical zeros, still. Set bounds=[(0, 10.0)]*n. Let's detail fast convolution score:
def fftconvolve_full(x):
    n = len(x)
    size = 1
    # Use scipy.fft? import numpy.fft. Need next power of two >=2n-1.
    m = 2*n -1
    L = 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
    X = np.fft.rfft(x, L)
    C = np.fft.irfft(X*X, L)[:m]
    # numerical imaginary? irfft real
    C[C<0 & C>-1e-12]=0
    C = np.clip(C,0,None)
    return C

Implement formula:
def valid_correlate(v, a):
    n=len(a); m=len(v)
    L=1 << (m+n-2).bit_length()
    V=np.fft.rfft(v, L)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(V*A, L)[:m+n-1]
    return c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
Check v signed; c real.

So:
def valid_correlate(v,a):
    vr=v[::-1]
    L=next_power_of_two(len(vr)+len(a)-1)
    c=irfft(rfft(vr,L)*rfft(a,L), L)[:len(vr)+len(a)-1]
    return c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]

Gradient step function:
def gradient_step(u, f, c, mode, beta, lam_list):
    n=len(f); mean=1.0/(2*n-1)
    if mode=='var':
       v = c - mean
    elif mode=='peak':
       q = beta * (c - mean) # focus above mean
       q = q - q.max(); w=np.exp(q); w/=w.sum()
       v = w
    elif mode=='peak_top':
       w=np.zeros_like(c); top=np.sort(c)[-K:]? Use indices. Set w_c = 1 for top K; maybe weight by c-c_med. Normalize.
    H=valid_correlate(v,f)
    Q=np.dot(v,c)  # if v signed, Q maybe var; for w positive Q.
    d=H - Q
    # For natural with f factor:
    d1 = f*d
    # We can try both: direct and f factor.
    best_cand=None
    for dvec in [d, d1]:
       sd=np.std(dvec)
       if sd<1e-15: continue
       dnorm=dvec/sd
       for lam in lam_list:
           u_new = u - lam*dnorm  # descent
           f_new=softmax(u_new)
           c_new=conv(f_new)
           score=2n*max(c_new)
           if score < best_score: update
    return

LP call:
from scipy.optimize import linprog
def lp_direction(f, T):
  A=build_conv_matrix(f)
  c=-np.ones(n)
  b=np.full(2*n-1, T)
  eps=1e-6
  ub=1.0
  bounds=[(eps, ub)]*n
  res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
  if not res.success: return None
  g=res.x
  # Check constraints? maybe due to tolerance.
  sum_g=g.sum()
  if sum_g <= n*eps*1.1: return None
  # regularize
  g = (1-0.05)*g/sum_g + 0.05/n
  g = np.clip(g,0,None); g/=g.sum()
  return g

Lower bound M≥S²/(2n-1)=1/(2n-1)=0.000488, so T not below. eps safe.

However, ub=1 may be too low if normalized g needs sum1 and before normalization sum small. LP maximizes sum under T; maximum sum maybe around T / min_support_weight. For f uniform-like, central constraint sum g ≤T; with eps lower, max sum ≈T (if central row all f positive). T~0.0005, sum_g 0.0005. ub1 no issue. Normalized g may be okay.

Softmax function:
def softmax_from_u(u):
    m=u.max()
    x=np.exp(u-m)
    x/=x.sum()
    return x

   # remove mean? not necessary but keep scale: u -= u.mean() to avoid drift

Pseudo for optimize_sequence(f0, u0, budget_seconds, rng):
 f = f0.copy(); u = u0.copy(); best_f=f.copy(); best_score=score_sum1(f)
 stalling=0; last_improve_time=now
 for iteration:
   if time > budget: break
   # compute c for current f (maybe from previous to save? We can keep c)
   c = get_conv(f); M=c.max(); score=2n*M
   if score < best_score: update best, last_improve
   else stalling += 1
   # gradient steps
   improved = False
   # variance mode
   if iteration % 2 == 0:
      cand, score_c, u_new = gradient_trial(u,f,c,'var', beta none, rng)
      if cand is not None and score_c < score:
         f=u? accept improved=True
   # peak mode
   if not improved:
      cand = gradient_trial(... 'peak', beta=peak_beta)
      if accept...
   # block random
   if not improved and iteration % 5 ==0:
      f_new = f * np.exp(rng.normal(0, sigma, n)); normalize; score; accept if improves
   # if stalling > 20: try LP
   if stalling > 20:
      g = lp_direction(f, T)
      if g is not None:
         line search direction; accept if improves
      reset stalling
   # keep f positive: f = np.maximum(f,1e-8); normalize
   # update u=log(f)-log(f).mean
 return best_f,best_score

Let's implement gradient_trial:
def gradient_trial(u, f, c, mode, beta, lam_list, rng):
    n=len(f)
    mean=1.0/(2*n-1)
    if mode == 'var':
       v = c - mean
    else:
       q = beta*(c - mean)
       q = q - q.max()
       w = np.exp(q)
       s = w.sum()
       if s <= 0: return None
       w /= s
       v = w
    H = correl(v, f)
    Q = float(np.dot(v,c))
    d = H - Q
    # Could center d? For softmax, adding constant doesn't matter. subtract mean maybe.
    # Try both scaled variants: d and f*d (but f*d = d elementwise product). If using f factor, for very small f no effect? Actually d*f.
    best_score = np.inf; best_f=None; best_u=None
    # Choose a few lam based on std
    variants = 2 if n <= 2048 else 1
    for variant in range(variants):
       if variant==0:
           dv = d
       else:
           dv = f*d
       sd = np.std(dv)
       if sd < 1e-15: continue
       dv_norm = dv / sd
       # Try lam values
       for lam in lam_list:
          u_new = u - lam * dv_norm
          # keep range limited: if std(u_new)>50 renormalize? softmax handles
          f_new = softmax(u_new)
          c_new = get_conv(f_new)
          sc = 2*n*c_new.max()
          if sc < best_score:
             best... 
    return best_f,best_score,best_u

For nonuniform, f factor may reduce extremes. Natural gradient theory suggests f*d. Use natural (f*d) only. Then lam std.

lp_direction(f,T,active):
  A = np.zeros((K,n))
  fr=f[::-1]
  for row,k in enumerate(active):
     j0=max(0,k-n+1); j1=min(n-1,k); start=n-1-k+j0; length=j1-j0+1
     A[row,j0:j1+1]=fr[start:start+length]
  bounds eps ub
  solve.
This is fast. Need active unique sorted? Use argpartition c to get top K. For reproducibility.

_g_beta = 1.0
_g_n = 1
def _mf_fun(u):
    a = softmax(u)
    c = conv_sum1(a)
    return logsumexp(_g_beta*c)/_g_beta
def _mf_jac(u):
    a=softmax(u); c=conv_sum1(a)
    q=_g_beta*c; ...
    H=valid_correlate(w,a)
    ga=(2.0/_g_beta)*H
    return a*ga - a*(float(np.dot(a,ga)))
Then call minimize(_mf_fun, u0, jac=_mf_jac, method='L-BFGS-B').

def refine_exact(a, budget, rng):
   u=log(a)
   best_score=score_sum1(a); best_f=a.copy()
   for it:
     a=softmax(u); c=conv(a); score=...
     update
     # Try small block multiplicative perturbations
     for sigma in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0]: maybe too many.
       u_p = u + sigma*rng.normal(0,1,n); u_p-=u_p.mean()
       a_p=softmax(u_p); sc=...
       accept best if sc<score.
     # Try gradient direction with active top weights (one step line search)
     v = weights from top K peaks (w = (c - mean)_+ normalized or softmax beta)
     H=correl(w,a); Q=...
     d = H - Q
     try lam [0.01,0.05,0.2,1]
   return best

Top-level imports:
import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import linprog, minimize

Maybe import scipy.fft next_fast_len; if unavailable? Use numpy bit length power-of-two for reliability.

Helper next_power_of_two(m):
  if m<=1 return1
  return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()

conv_sum1(a):
  n=a.size
  L=next_power_of_two(2*n-1)
  A=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
  c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)[:2*n-1]
  # due to roundoff negative tiny
  c = np.where(c < 0, 0.0, c)
  return c

score_sum1(a):
  # a positive? we can normalize? Assume sum 1. But for safety:
  s=a.sum()
  if s <=0: return inf
  a2=a/s
  c=conv_sum1(a2)
  return float(2*len(a2)*c.max())

Softmax:
def softmax(u):
  u = u - np.max(u) # not mean; max for stability
  x=np.exp(u)
  s=x.sum()
  if s==0 or not finite: return np.ones_like(u)/u.size
  return x/s
For centering, in objective we center mean; softmax max stable.

valid_correlate(v,a):
 n=len(a); L=next_power_of_two(len(v)+n-1); vv=v[::-1]; C=irfft(rfft(vv)*rfft(a), L)[:len(v)+n-1]; return C[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]

Objective globals:
_g_beta = 1.0
_g_n = 1
def _mf_fun(u):
   u = u - np.mean(u)
   a = softmax(u)
   c = conv_sum1(a)
   q = _g_beta * c
   m = np.max(q)
   return float((m + np.log(np.sum(np.exp(q-m)))) / _g_beta)
def _mf_jac(u):
   u = u - np.mean(u)
   a = softmax(u)
   c = conv_sum1(a)
   q = _g_beta * c
   m = np.max(q)
   ex = np.exp(q-m)
   w = ex / ex.sum()
   H = valid_correlate(w, a)
   ga = (2.0/_g_beta)*H
   # natural gradient for softmax
   return a*ga - a*(float(np.dot(a,ga)))
Check scale: For beta large, ga=2/beta*H small; L-BFGS step maybe small. But objective scale ~1/beta*logsum, gradient small. L-BFGS okay? It may take many steps. We can scale objective by n? The gradient magnitude: H~1/n, ga~2/(beta n). If beta~10/(mean)=20n, ga~2/(20n*n)=1/n² ~1e-5 for n=256. Objective scale similarly small. L-BFGS may be slow due to tiny gradients. We can multiply objective and gradient by factor (e.g., n²) without changing minimizer. Use _g_bias? Define beta but scale_obj = 1.0 * n*n? In fun return F*n*n; jac return grad*n*n. This increases magnitude. Let's set scale = float(n*n) inside? We can use global _g_scale. But n known. In _mf_fun: return F * _g_scale. In _mf_jac: grad * _g_scale. Set _g_scale = n*n. This makes gradient O(1). Good.

Set globals before minimize:
_g_beta = beta
_g_scale = float(n*n)

But _mf_jac uses _g_scale.

Exact refine implementation:
def exact_refine(a0, budget, rng):
   n=len(a0); best=a0.copy(); best_score=score_sum1(best)
   u=np.log(np.maximum(best,1e-300)); u-=u.mean()
   best_u=u.copy()
   stall=0
  for it:
    if time > deadline: break
    a=best.copy()
    score=best_score
    c=conv_sum1(a); # score unnormalized? sum1.
    # Generate trials list
    trials=[]
    # top-weight gradient
    w = peak_weights(c, mode)
    H=valid_correlate(w,a); Q=dot(w,c); d=H-Q
    for variant in [d, a*d]:
      dstd=np.std(variant); if >0:
         for lam in [0.01,0.05,0.2,1.0]:
            u_new=u - lam*variant/dstd
            a_new=softmax(u_new); sc=score_sum1(a_new); trials...
    # random log perturbations
    for sigma in [0.05,0.2,0.5,1.0]:
       u_new = u + sigma*rng.normal(0,1,n); center; a_new=softmax; trial
    # block multiplicative: choose block length n//32, multiply exp noise per block
    for b in [8,32,128]: width=n//b; noise=rng.normal(0,0.2, width); a_new=a*np.repeat(np.exp(noise),width); normalize; trial
    Accept best trial if sc < score - 1e-12:
       best=a_trial; best_score=sc; best_u=u_trial; stall=0
    else:
       stall +=1
       if stall > 5: add small noise to u to escape: u = u + 0.1*rng.normal; center
  return best,best_score

Check gradient correctness for softmax parameterization and F=(1/β)logΣe^{βc}. We did g_u = a*(g_a - avg). Is this the Euclidean gradient w.r.t u or natural? Let's verify with chain rule: a_i = e^{u_i}/S. ∂a_i/∂u_j = a_i δ_ij - a_i a_j. ∂F/∂u_j = Σ_i ∂F/∂a_i (a_j δ_ij - a_j a_i) = a_j ∂F/∂a_j - a_j Σ_i a_i ∂F/∂a_i. Yes. Good. This is true gradient, not natural approx. Sum zero. Good.

def refine_exact(a0, budget, rng):
   n=len(a0); t_end=...
   best_a=a0.copy(); best_score=score_sum1(best_a)
   u=np.log(np.maximum(best_a,1e-300)); u-=u.mean()
   best_u=u.copy()
   mean_inv=2*n-1
   # Try a few L-BFGS high beta from best_u and random u
   # But to avoid many, do while time:
   while time.monotonic() < t_end:
       # one L-BFGS from current best with high beta maybe 50*mean_inv
       for beta_target in [20,50,100]:
           if time >= t_end: break
           u0 = best_u + 0.2*rng.normal(0,1,n) if rng.random()<0.5 else best_u.copy()
           u0-=u0.mean()
           _g_beta=beta_target*mean_inv; _g_scale=float(n*n)
           enable callback
           try: res=minimize(_mf_fun,u0,jac=_mf_jac,method='L-BFGS-B',options={'maxiter': max(50, min(300, int((t_end-time)/10))),...})
           except: res=None
           disable
           # evaluate callback best and res.x
           for cand: score update
       # random exact perturbations
       if time >= t_end: break
       for sigma in [0.05,0.1,0.3,1.0]:
          u_new = best_u + sigma*rng.normal(0,1,n); center
          a_new=softmax(u_new); sc=score_sum1(a_new)
          if sc<best_score: update best and u_new
       # block perturbation
       B=32; width=(n+B-1)//B? Use np.repeat noise.
       for sigma in [0.1,0.3]:
          noise = np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma, max(1,n//32)))  # length maybe ~32
          a_new = best_a * np.repeat(noise, n)//? Need same length: noise length m = ceil(n/m); use np.repeat, truncate to n.
          a_new=np.concatenate... simpler: block_len = n//m +1.
          a_new = np.repeat(noise, block_len)[:n]
          a_new = best_a * a_new; normalize
       # if stalled, add bigger noise
   return best

def top_weights(c, n, K=64):
  # Focus on top K above mean
  mean=1/(2n-1)
  # Use softmax beta maybe easier
  beta = 20/(mean)
  q=beta*(c-mean); q-=q.max(); w=np.exp(q); w/=w.sum()
  return w

Now, local_hill_climb implementation details:
def local_hill_climb(a0, t_end, rng):
    n=len(a0)
    best_a = np.clip(a0,0,None); s=best_a.sum(); if s<=0 best_a=ones/n else best_a=s...; best_score=score_sum1(best_a)
    u = np.log(np.maximum(best_a,1e-300)); u -= u.mean(); best_u=u.copy()
    stall=0; it=0
    mean = 1/(2*n-1)
    while time.monotonic() < t_end:
        it+=1
        c = conv_sum1(best_a); score=2n*c.max()  # same as best_score maybe
        # If score smaller due to numerical, update
        if score < best_score: best...
        trials=[]
        # gradient weights
        beta = 25.0 / mean  # q target 25
        q = beta * (c - mean); q -= np.max(q); w=np.exp(q); w/=w.sum()
        H = valid_correlate(w,best_a); Q=float(np.dot(w,c)); d=H-Q
        # Use natural direction: dnat = best_a*d
        for dvec in (d, best_a*d):
            ds=np.std(dvec)
            if ds>1e-16:
                dnorm=dvec/ds
                for lam in (0.01,0.05,0.2,1.0):
                    u_new=u - lam*dnorm; a_new=softmax(u_new); sc=score_sum1(a_new)
                    trials.append((sc,a_new,u_new))
        # random log perturb
        for sigma in (0.05,0.2,0.8):
            u_new=u + sigma*rng.standard_normal(n); u_new-=u_new.mean(); a_new=softmax(u_new); sc=score_sum1(a_new); trials...
        # block
        for m in (8,32):
            block_len = max(1, n//m + 1)
            noise = np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.2,m))
            mult = np.repeat(noise, block_len)[:n]
            a_new = best_a * mult; s=a_new.sum(); if s>0 a_new/=s; sc=score_sum1(a_new); u_new=np.log(max(a_new,1e-300)); center; trials
        # choose best trial
        best_trial=None
        for sc, a_c, u_c in trials:
            if np.isfinite(sc) and (best_trial is None or sc < best_trial[0]): best_trial=(sc,a_c,u_c)
        if best_trial is not None and best_trial[0] < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score,best_a,best_u=...
            stall=0
        else:
            stall +=1
            if stall > 10:
                # larger random restart around best
                sigma = 0.5 + 0.5*stall*0.1? cap 3
                u = best_u + 0.3*rng.standard_normal(n); u-=u.mean()
                # maybe keep best separate; use u for next but do not change best until improvement. This is exploratory.
        # To avoid evaluating huge trials if n large, if it%20==0 and best_score<1.1 break? no.
    return best_a,best_score

def resize_to_u(a, n):
   if a is None or len(a)==0: return None
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float); a=np.clip(a,0,None); s=a.sum(); if s<=0 return None; a/=s
   if a.size == n: u=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300)); u-=u.mean(); return u
   xs=np.linspace(0,1,a.size); ys=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   an=np.interp(ys,xs,a); an=np.maximum(an,1e-12); an/=an.sum()
   u=np.log(an); u-=u.mean(); return u

Let's design minimal LP helper:
def lp_direction(f, c, T, K=128):
   n=len(f)
   if T <=0: return None
   # active top K c
   if c.size <= K: active=np.arange(c.size); k_arr=active
   else:
      idx=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
   A=np.zeros((len(idx), n))
   fr=f[::-1]
   for row,k in enumerate(idx):
      k=int(k)
      j0=max(0,k-n+1); j1=min(n-1,k)
      if j1<j0: continue
      start=n-1-k+j0
      length=j1-j0+1
      A[row, j0:j1+1] = fr[start:start+length]
   c_obj=-np.ones(n)
   b=np.full(len(idx), T)
   eps=1e-8; ub=1.0
   bounds=[(eps, ub)]*n
   try: res=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
   except Exception: return None
   if not res.success: return None
   g=np.asarray(res.x,float)
   sg=g.sum()
   if sg <= n*eps*1.1: return None
   g = 0.9*(g/sg) + 0.1/n
   g=np.clip(g,0,None); s=g.sum(); if s<=0 return None; g/=s
   return g

def lp_polish(a0,t_end,rng):
  best=a0; best_score=...
  it=0
  while time<t_end:
    c=conv(best); M=c.max(); score=...
    for frac in [0.9,0.8,0.7]:
       T=M*frac
       g=lp_direction(best,c,T,K=128)
       if g is None: continue
       for lam in [0.1,0.3,0.5,0.8,1.0]:
          a_new=(1-lam)*best+lam*g; normalize; sc=score_sum1; if sc<best_score update
    if no improvement in this iter: break or random noise
  return best

```
def lp_polish(a0,t_end,rng,max_iter=50):
   best=...; stall=0
   for it in range(max_iter):
     if time>t_end: break
     c=conv(best); score=...
     improved=False
     M=c.max()
     active_K = 64 if len(best)>512 else 128
     for Tfrac in [0.95,0.85,0.75,0.6]:
       g=lp_direction(best,c,M*Tfrac,active_K)
       if g is None: continue
       if stall>20 and rng.random()<0.5: g = 0.5*g+0.5*(rng.normal? maybe uniform)
       for lam in [0.2,0.5,0.8,1.0]:
          a_new=...
          sc=score_sum1
          if sc<best_score: update; improved=True
     if not improved:
       # try random block or break
       if it%10==0:
          a_new=... random; if improves update
       else: stall++; if stall>30: break
   return best
```

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative sequence minimizing

    2 * n * max(k * a) / (sum(a))**2

where k = full linear convolution a * a.

Method:
  - Work on the simplex (sum(a) = 1) using softmax parameters u.
  - Use multi-start L-BFGS on a smooth peak objective:
        (1/beta) log sum_k exp(beta * (a*a)_k)
    with an analytic gradient.
  - Several sequence lengths are explored; the best exact score is kept.
  - Polish with a few LP-driven directions and exact-score hill climbing.
"""

import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize, linprog

# Globals used by the smooth objective and its Jacobian.
_g_beta = 1.0
_g_scale = 1.0

# Global state for L-BFGS callback, used to track exact-score improvements.
_cb_state = {
    "enabled": False,
    "best_score": np.inf,
    "best_a": None,
    "count": 0,
}


def next_power_of_two(m: int) -> int:
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (int(m) - 1).bit_length()


def conv_sum1(a: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Full linear convolution of a with itself, assuming a is non-negative."""
    n = int(a.size)
    L = next_power_of_two(2 * n - 1)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, L)[: 2 * n - 1]
    np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    return c


def score_sum1(a: np.ndarray) -> float:
    """Score for a normalized or unnormalized non-negative vector."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.inf
    a = a / s
    c = conv_sum1(a)
    return float(2.0 * a.size * float(np.max(c)))


def softmax(u: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    u = np.asarray(u, dtype=float)
    u = u - float(np.max(u))
    x = np.exp(u)
    s = float(np.sum(x))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones_like(u) / float(u.size)
    return x / s


def valid_correlate(v: np.ndarray, a: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Return H[i] = sum_j v[i+j] * a[j] for i = 0..n-1,
    where v has length 2n-1 and a has length n.
    """
    n = int(a.size)
    L = next_power_of_two(len(v) + n - 1)
    vr = v[::-1]
    cr = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(vr, L) * np.fft.rfft(a, L), L)[: len(v) + n - 1]
    return cr[n - 1 : 2 * n - 1][::-1].copy()


def _mf_fun(u: np.ndarray) -> float:
    u = np.asarray(u, dtype=float)
    u = u - float(np.mean(u))
    a = softmax(u)
    c = conv_sum1(a)
    q = _g_beta * c
    m = float(np.max(q))
    val = (m + float(np.log(np.sum(np.exp(q - m))))) / _g_beta
    return float(val * _g_scale)


def _mf_jac(u: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    u = np.asarray(u, dtype=float)
    u = u - float(np.mean(u))
    a = softmax(u)
    c = conv_sum1(a)
    q = _g_beta * c
    m = float(np.max(q))
    ex = np.exp(q - m)
    w = ex / float(np.sum(ex))
    H = valid_correlate(w, a)
    ga = (2.0 / _g_beta) * H
    # Chain rule for softmax parameterization.
    grad = a * ga - a * float(np.dot(a, ga))
    return grad * _g_scale


def _lf_callback(xk: np.ndarray) -> None:
    st = _cb_state
    if not st.get("enabled", False):
        return
    st["count"] += 1
    if st["count"] % 20 != 0:
        return
    xk = np.asarray(xk, dtype=float)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(xk)):
        return
    a = softmax(xk)
    sc = score_sum1(a)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < st["best_score"]:
        st["best_score"] = float(sc)
        st["best_a"] = a.copy()


def random_start(n: int, rng: np.random.Generator) -> np.ndarray:
    """Generate a random softmax log-parameter vector of length n."""
    kind = int(rng.integers(0, 4))

    if kind == 0:
        sigma = float(rng.choice([0.3, 0.7, 1.0, 2.0]))
        u = sigma * rng.standard_normal(n)
        if rng.random() < 0.5:
            u = 0.7 * u + 0.3 * np.convolve(u, np.ones(3) / 3.0, mode="same")

    elif kind == 1:
        p = float(rng.choice([0.2, 0.35, 0.5, 0.65, 0.8]))
        b = (rng.random(n) < p).astype(float)
        b += 1e-3
        u = np.log(b)

    elif kind == 2:
        B = int(rng.integers(4, 65))
        sigma = float(rng.choice([0.5, 1.0, 2.0]))
        h = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, B)) + 1e-4
        h /= h.sum()
        block_len = n // B + 1
        a = np.repeat(h, block_len)[:n]
        a += 1e-8
        a /= a.sum()
        u = np.log(a)

    else:
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
        u = np.zeros(n)
        for k in range(1, 6):
            amp = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0))
            phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
            u += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * k * x + phase)
        u += rng.normal(0.0, 0.1, n)

    u = u - float(np.mean(u))
    return u


def resize_to_u(a: np.ndarray, n: int):
    """Convert a positive sequence to a centered log-softmax vector of length n."""
    if a is None:
        return None
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float)
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    a = a / s
    if a.size == n:
        u = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
    else:
        xs = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size)
        ys = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
        a2 = np.interp(ys, xs, a)
        a2 = np.maximum(a2, 1e-12)
        a2 /= a2.sum()
        u = np.log(a2)
    u = u - float(np.mean(u))
    return u


def run_l_bfgs(n: int, budget: float, rng: np.random.Generator, initial_points=None):
    """Multi-start L-BFGS on the smooth peak objective."""
    if budget <= 0:
        return None, np.inf

    global _g_beta, _g_scale

    t_end = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget))

    fixed_starts = []
    for _ in range(3):
        fixed_starts.append(np.zeros(n, dtype=float))

    if initial_points:
        for p in initial_points:
            if len(fixed_starts) >= 8:
                break
            p = np.asarray(p, dtype=float)
            if p.size != n:
                continue
            p = p - float(np.mean(p))
            if np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
                fixed_starts.append(p.copy())

    beta_cycle = [4.0, 10.0, 25.0, 60.0]

    if n <= 256:
        max_total = 120
        base_maxiter = 900
    elif n <= 512:
        max_total = 60
        base_maxiter = 450
    else:
        max_total = 30
        base_maxiter = 220

    best_a = None
    best_score = np.inf

    def consider(a, sc):
        nonlocal best_a, best_score
        if a is None or not np.isfinite(sc):
            return
        if sc < best_score:
            best_score = float(sc)
            best_a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float).copy()

    for it in range(max_total):
        if time.monotonic() >= t_end:
            break

        if it < len(fixed_starts):
            u0 = fixed_starts[it].copy()
        else:
            u0 = random_start(n, rng)

        if not np.all(np.isfinite(u0)):
            continue

        # Record exact score of the starting point.
        a0 = softmax(u0)
        consider(a0, score_sum1(a0))

        beta_target = beta_cycle[it % len(beta_cycle)]
        _g_beta = beta_target * float(2 * n - 1)
        _g_scale = 10.0 * float(n) * float(n)

        _cb_state["enabled"] = True
        _cb_state["best_score"] = np.inf
        _cb_state["best_a"] = None
        _cb_state["count"] = 0

        options = {
            "maxiter": int(base_maxiter),
            "maxfun": int(base_maxiter * 25),
            "ftol": 1e-12,
            "gtol": 1e-5,
            "disp": False,
        }

        try:
            res = minimize(
                _mf_fun,
                u0,
                jac=_mf_jac,
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                callback=_lf_callback,
                options=options,
            )
        except Exception:
            res = None
        finally:
            _cb_state["enabled"] = False

        if _cb_state.get("best_a") is not None:
            consider(_cb_state["best_a"], _cb_state.get("best_score", np.inf))

        if res is not None:
            x = getattr(res, "x", None)
            if x is not None:
                x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
                if np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
                    a1 = softmax(x)
                    consider(a1, score_sum1(a1))

        if it % 15 == 0:
            print(
                f"  L-BFGS n={n} it={it:+04d} best={best_score:.6f} t_left={t_end-time.monotonic():6.1f}s",
                flush=True,
            )

    if best_a is None:
        # Safe fallback: uniform length n.
        return None, np.inf

    a = np.clip(best_a, 0.0, None)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return None, np.inf
    return a / s, float(best_score)


def lp_direction(f: np.ndarray, c: np.ndarray, T: float, K: int = 128):
    """
    SOTA-style LP direction:
      maximize 1^T g
      subject to (f * g)[k] <= T for top K lags,  g >= 0
    Returns a normalized non-negative vector or None.
    """
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=float)
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=float)
    n = int(f.size)
    if T <= 1e-300 or n <= 0:
        return None

    m = c.size
    K = max(1, min(int(K), m))
    if K >= m:
        idx = np.arange(m)
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]

    A = np.zeros((K, n), dtype=float)
    fr = f[::-1]

    for r, k in enumerate(idx):
        k = int(k)
        j0 = max(0, k - n + 1)
        j1 = min(n - 1, k)
        if j1 < j0:
            continue
        start = n - 1 - k + j0
        length = j1 - j0 + 1
        A[r, j0 : j1 + 1] = fr[start : start + length]

    objective = -np.ones(n, dtype=float)
    b = np.full(K, T, dtype=float)

    eps = 1e-8
    ub = 1.0
    bounds = [(eps, ub)] * n

    try:
        res = linprog(objective, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method="highs")
    except Exception:
        return None

    if res is None or not getattr(res, "success", False):
        return None

    g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
        return None

    sg = float(np.sum(g))
    if sg <= n * eps * 1.5:
        return None

    # Regularize to avoid one-hot LP vertices.
    g = 0.90 * (g / sg) + 0.10 / n
    g = np.clip(g, 0.0, None)
    sg = float(np.sum(g))
    if sg <= 1e-300:
        return None
    g = g / sg
    return g


def lp_polish(a0: np.ndarray, t_end: float, rng: np.random.Generator):
    """Try a few LP-guided exact-score improvements."""
    if a0 is None:
        return a0, np.inf

    a0 = np.asarray(a0, dtype=float)
    a0 = np.clip(a0, 0.0, None)
    s = a0.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
        a0 = np.ones(64, dtype=float) / 64.0
    else:
        a0 = a0 / s

    n = int(a0.size)
    best_a = a0.copy()
    best_score = score_sum1(best_a)
    stall = 0

    max_iter = 60 if n <= 512 else 35
    active_K = 128 if n <= 512 else 64

    for it in range(max_iter):
        if time.monotonic() >= t_end:
            break

        c = conv_sum1(best_a)
        sc = float(2 * n * np.max(c))
        if sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_a = best_a.copy()

        M = float(np.max(c))
        improved = False

        for frac in (0.95, 0.85, 0.75, 0.60):
            g = lp_direction(best_a, c, M * frac, active_K)
            if g is None:
                continue

            for lam in (0.20, 0.50, 0.80, 1.00):
                a_new = (1.0 - lam) * best_a + lam * g
                a_new = np.clip(a_new, 0.0, None)
                ss = float(a_new.sum())
                if ss <= 1e-300:
                    continue
                a_new = a_new / ss
                sc_new = score_sum1(a_new)
                if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_score:
                    best_score = float(sc_new)
                    best_a = a_new
                    stall = 0
                    improved = True

        if not improved:
            stall += 1
            if it % 10 == 0:
                # Exact-score block perturbation.
                m = 32
                block_len = n // m + 1
                noise = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.3, m))
                mult = np.repeat(noise, block_len)[:n]
                a_new = best_a * mult
                ss = float(a_new.sum())
                if ss > 1e-300:
                    a_new = np.clip(a_new, 0.0, None) / ss
                    sc_new = score_sum1(a_new)
                    if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_score:
                        best_score = float(sc_new)
                        best_a = a_new
                        stall = 0

            if stall > 45:
                break

    return best_a, float(best_score)


def local_hill_climb(a0: np.ndarray, t_end: float, rng: np.random.Generator):
    """Exact-score local search with soft-max/log perturbations and peak-gradient steps."""
    if a0 is None:
        return a0, np.inf

    a0 = np.asarray(a0, dtype=float)
    a0 = np.clip(a0, 0.0, None)
    s = a0.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
        a0 = np.ones(64, dtype=float) / 64.0
    else:
        a0 = a0 / s

    n = int(a0.size)
    best_a = a0.copy()
    best_score = float(score_sum1(best_a))

    u = np.log(np.maximum(best_a, 1e-300))
    u -= float(np.mean(u))

    if n <= 256:
        max_iter = 5000
        grad_lams = (0.01, 0.05, 0.20, 1.00)
        rand_sigmas_small = (0.05, 0.20, 0.80)
        rand_sigmas_large = (0.20, 1.00, 3.00)
        block_trials = ((16, 0.2), (32, 0.5), (64, 0.8))
    elif n <= 512:
        max_iter = 2200
        grad_lams = (0.02, 0.10, 0.50)
        rand_sigmas_small = (0.05, 0.20, 0.80)
        rand_sigmas_large = (0.20, 1.00, 3.00)
        block_trials = ((16, 0.2), (32, 0.5))
    else:
        max_iter = 700
        grad_lams = (0.05, 0.50)
        rand_sigmas_small = (0.10, 0.50)
        rand_sigmas_large = (0.30, 1.00)
        block_trials = ((32, 0.3), (64, 0.6))

    stall = 0

    for it in range(max_iter):
        if time.monotonic() >= t_end:
            break

        c = conv_sum1(best_a)
        sc = float(2 * n * np.max(c))
        if sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_a = best_a.copy()

        trial_score = best_score
        trial_a = None
        trial_u = None

        def accept_candidate(a_new, u_new, sc_new):
            nonlocal trial_score, trial_a, trial_u
            a_new = np.asarray(a_new, dtype=float)
            if a_new.size != n:
                return
            if not np.all(np.isfinite(a_new)):
                return
            a_new = np.clip(a_new, 0.0, None)
            ss = float(a_new.sum())
            if ss <= 1e-300:
                return
            a_new = a_new / ss
            u_new = np.asarray(u_new, dtype=float)
            if not np.all(np.isfinite(u_new)):
                return
            if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < trial_score:
                trial_score = float(sc_new)
                trial_a = a_new
                trial_u = u_new - float(np.mean(u_new))

        # Peak-weighted gradient direction.
        mean_c = 1.0 / float(2 * n - 1)
        beta_target = 25.0 if best_score > 1.45 else 55.0
        beta = beta_target * float(2 * n - 1)
        q = beta * (c - mean_c)
        q -= float(np.max(q))
        w = np.exp(q)
        sw = float(np.sum(w))
        if sw > 1e-300:
            w = w / sw
            H = valid_correlate(w, best_a)
            Q = float(np.dot(w, c))
            d0 = H - Q
            d1 = best_a * d0
            for dvec in (d0, d1):
                sd = float(np.std(dvec))
                if sd <= 1e-15:
                    continue
                dnorm = dvec / sd
                for lam in grad_lams:
                    u_new = u - lam * dnorm
                    a_new = softmax(u_new)
                    sc_new = score_sum1(a_new)
                    accept_candidate(a_new, u_new, sc_new)

        # Random log perturbations.
        sigmas = rand_sigmas_small if stall <= 20 else rand_sigmas_large
        for sigma in sigmas:
            u_new = u + sigma * rng.standard_normal(n)
            a_new = softmax(u_new)
            sc_new = score_sum1(a_new)
            accept_candidate(a_new, u_new, sc_new)

        # Block multiplicative perturbations.
        for m, sigma in block_trials:
            m = max(1, min(m, n))
            block_len = n // m + 1
            noise = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, m))
            mult = np.repeat(noise, block_len)[:n]
            a_new = best_a * mult
            ss = float(a_new.sum())
            if ss <= 1e-300:
                continue
            u_new = np.log(np.maximum(a_new, 1e-300))
            sc_new = score_sum1(a_new)
            accept_candidate(a_new, u_new, sc_new)

        if trial_a is not None and trial_score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = float(trial_score)
            best_a = trial_a
            u = trial_u
            stall = 0
        else:
            stall += 1
            if stall > 50:
                # Periodic is not required; keep stalling for larger random sigmas.
                pass

    return best_a, float(best_score)


def propose_candidate(seed: int = 42, budget_s: int = 1800, **kwargs):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start_time + max(60.0, float(budget_s)) - 4.0

    global_best_a = None
    global_best_score = np.inf
    global_best_n = 0

    # If the provided previous sequence is available, use it as one interpolation source.
    provided = None
    try:
        provided = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=float)
    except Exception:
        provided = None
    if provided is not None:
        provided = np.nan_to_num(provided, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        provided = np.clip(provided, 0.0, None)
        if provided.size == 0 or provided.sum() <= 1e-300:
            provided = None

    plan = [
        (256, 0.16),
        (512, 0.36),
        (1024, 0.36),
    ]
    base_budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s) - 8.0)

    for idx, (n, frac) in enumerate(plan):
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining < 40.0:
            break

        alloc = frac * base_budget
        # Do not use more than most of what remains except for the last planned run.
        if idx < len(plan) - 1:
            alloc = min(alloc, remaining * 0.90)
        alloc = max(25.0, alloc)

        end = min(time.monotonic() + alloc, deadline - 30.0)
        budget = max(0.0, end - time.monotonic())
        if budget <= 0.0:
            break

        initial_points = []

        if idx == 0 and provided is not None:
            up = resize_to_u(provided, n)
            if up is not None:
                initial_points.append(up)

        if global_best_a is not None:
            up = resize_to_u(global_best_a, n)
            if up is not None:
                initial_points.append(up)

        print(f"Explore length n={n}, budget={budget:.1f}s", flush=True)
        a_cur, sc_cur = run_l_bfgs(n, budget, rng, initial_points)

        if a_cur is not None and np.isfinite(sc_cur) and sc_cur < global_best_score:
            global_best_a = a_cur.copy()
            global_best_score = float(sc_cur)
            global_best_n = n
            print(f"  new best n={n} score={global_best_score:.6f}", flush=True)

    # Final polishing on the current best sequence.
    if global_best_a is not None:
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining > 20.0:
            print(
                f"Polishing n={global_best_n} score={global_best_score:.6f}, remaining={remaining:.1f}s",
                flush=True,
            )

            # A brief LP polish can make a large jump; give it a bounded slice.
            if remaining > 60.0:
                lp_end = time.monotonic() + min(300.0, remaining * 0.35)
                a_lp, sc_lp = lp_polish(global_best_a, lp_end, rng)
                if a_lp is not None and np.isfinite(sc_lp) and sc_lp < global_best_score:
                    global_best_a = a_lp
                    global_best_score = float(sc_lp)
                    print(f"  LP polish score={global_best_score:.6f}", flush=True)

            # Exact-score hill climb with the rest of the time.
            if deadline - time.monotonic() > 5.0:
                a_fin, sc_fin = local_hill_climb(global_best_a, deadline - 2.0, rng)
                if a_fin is not None:
                    a_fin = np.clip(a_fin, 0.0, None)
                    s = a_fin.sum()
                    if s > 1e-300:
                        a_fin = a_fin / s
                        sc_exact = score_sum1(a_fin)
                        if np.isfinite(sc_exact) and sc_exact < global_best_score:
                            global_best_score = float(sc_exact)
                            global_best_a = a_fin

    # Ensure a valid fallback.
    if global_best_a is None:
        global_best_a = np.ones(128, dtype=float) / 128.0
        global_best_score = score_sum1(global_best_a)

    result = np.clip(np.asarray(global_best_a, dtype=float), 0.0, None)
    s = float(result.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        result = result / max(s, 1e-300)

    final_score = score_sum1(result)
    print(
        f"Final length={result.size} score={final_score:.6f} "
        f"time={time.monotonic()-start_time:.1f}s",
        flush=True,
    )

    return [float(x) for x in result]
```
